Five large circles are arranged in a cross pattern (top, bottom, left, right, and center). To the left of the left-most large circle, three smaller circles are arranged vertically. Text labels and astronomical data surround the diagram.</image-desc>

# 星體之圖
## 内屏之圖

<margin>太微垣</margin>

内屏一
火 水

<image-desc>A star chart diagram depicting the asterism "Neiping" (Inner Screen). Four small circular markers represent the stars, arranged in an irregular quadrilateral pattern. The labels indicate that this is the first star of the Inner Screen, associated with the elements Fire and Water, and located within the "Ji" (己) zodiacal palace. To the left, numerical coordinates specify its position in longitude and latitude relative to the celestial equator.</image-desc>

# 太子之圖

<image-desc>A historical astronomical diagram for the star 太子 (Crown Prince). A single black dot near the center-right of the frame represents the star. To the right of the dot is the main title and the star's distance classification. To the left of the dot are columns detailing the star's name, zodiacal palace, and precise astronomical coordinates including longitude, latitude, lunar mansion position, and magnitude.</image-desc>

The diagram features fifteen stars represented as small open circles, arranged in a descending triangular formation: one star in the top row, followed by rows of two, three, four, and five stars respectively. Above the stars are the labels "明堂" and "金水" (Metal-Water). Below the stars is the positional label "巳宮" (Si Palace). ### 靈臺之圖

<image-desc>
An astronomical diagram for the constellation "Lingtai" (靈臺 - Spirit Terrace). The diagram consists of six small circles representing stars. To the left of the diagram, there are vertical labels: "靈臺" (the constellation name), "六星" (indicating six stars), and "巳宮" (indicating its position in the Si palace or the Snake zodiac sign).
</image-desc>

The text "長垣 一" (Changyuan 1) is written to the left of the topmost star, and "巳宮" (Snake Palace) is written near the center of the grouping, indicating its position in the twelve palaces of the Chinese zodiac.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A traditional Chinese astronomical diagram of the "Three Terraces" (Santai) asterism. It consists of six stars arranged in three pairs, connected by a dotted line. The pairs are labeled from top to bottom with the characters "上" (Upper), "中" (Middle), and "下" (Lower) to indicate the Three Terraces.

A traditional Chinese star chart depicting the "Left Wall" (左垣) of the Heavenly Market Enclosure (天市垣). The diagram consists of eleven small circles representing stars, connected by a dotted line to form a wide arc. Each star is labeled with a single Chinese character identifying it. Starting from the top right and following the curve counter-clockwise and then down, the labels are: 魏 (Wei), 趙 (Zhao), 九 (Jiu), 河 (He), 市 (Shi), 海 (Hai), 燕 (Yan), 秦 (Qin), 蜀 (Shu), 巴 (Ba), and 南 (Nan).

<image-desc>An astronomical star chart depicting the "Shilou" (Market Tower) asterism. The diagram features six stars indicated by small circles, arranged in a rough circular or hexagonal loop. ## 星牀等

## 柱星之圖

附庫樓衡星并見

<image-desc>
A traditional Chinese astronomical chart within a rectangular border. The stars are depicted as small circles, with some connected by thin lines to form asterisms.
- Several distinct groups of stars are labeled "柱" (Zhu - Pillars). These are located at the top center, bottom center, and along the left side of the main cluster.
- A central group of four stars is labeled "衡" (Heng - Balance).
- To the right, a larger, semi-circular arrangement of stars is labeled "庫樓" (Ku Lou - Storage House).
The text is written in vertical columns to the right of the diagram.
</image-desc>

# 陽門之圖

<image-desc>An astronomical diagram titled "陽門之圖" (Map of Yangmen). It features a central arrangement of symbols: a small circle at the top, followed by a dot below it, another dot further down and slightly to the right, and a final dot at the bottom. To the right of the diagram, three vertical columns provide data: Latitude (緯度) as 19 degrees 37 minutes north of the equator (赤北一十九度三十七分), Lunar Mansion degree (宿度) as 11 degrees of Di (氐十一度), and Stellar Body (星體) classification as fourth magnitude (四等). To the left of the diagram, vertical text identifies the subject as "Yangmen" (陽門) with elemental associations of "Metal and Water" (金水), located in the "Mao Palace" (卯宮), with a further coordinate (緯度) of 214 degrees 22 minutes (二百一十四度二十二分).</image-desc>

The data includes the star's magnitude (星等), its position within a lunar mansion (宿度), and its equatorial coordinates: declination (緯度, specifically 赤南, south of the equator) and right ascension/longitude (經度). The small characters "火" and "土" refer to astrological associations, and "卯宮" indicates the zodiacal sign position.</meta>

<image-desc>
A traditional woodblock-printed star chart for the constellation Tiancang (天倉 - Celestial Granary). The diagram on the right depicts six stars, represented by small circles, connected by straight lines in a sequence that resembles a tilted, open container. The label "戌宮" (Xu Palace, corresponding to the zodiac sign Aries in this context) is written near the second star from the left. To the left of the diagram, vertical text provides the star's name and its astronomical coordinates.
</image-desc>

# 天狗之圖

<image-desc>A star chart for the "Tiangou" (Celestial Dog) asterism. The diagram features seven stars depicted as circles connected by lines in a hook-like configuration. Two of the stars contain the Western numerals '2' and '3'. Some circles are solid black, and others are hollow. The text labels are scattered around the diagram to indicate astronomical coordinates and classifications.</image-desc>

->星躔<-

->五等<-

# 天社之圖

<image-desc>An astronomical diagram representing the Tianshe (Celestial Earth God) constellation. It features six circular markers representing stars. Three markers are solid black and three are hollow. They are arranged in two clusters: on the left, a solid circle is connected by a diagonal line upward to a hollow circle, which then connects downward to a hollow circle. To the right of this group is a single solid circle, followed by another pair consisting of a hollow circle connected upward to a final hollow circle. The overall arrangement forms a series of peaks and valleys across the horizontal plane.</image-desc>

**天社一上**

**未宮**

**經度**

**一百一十九度五十六分**

# 軒轅之圖

->緯度　　宿度　　星等<-

->赤南二十八　張一度　六等<-

<image-desc>
A traditional Chinese star chart depicting the constellation Xuanyuan (軒轅), which corresponds to the modern constellation Leo. The diagram shows 17 stars represented as small circles—some solid and some dashed—connected by lines to form the characteristic sickle shape. To the right of the diagram, three vertical columns of text provide astronomical data: Latitude (緯度) as "赤南二十八" (South of the Equator 28), Mansion Degree (宿度) as "張一度" (1st degree of the Zhang mansion), and Star Magnitude (星等) as "六等" (6th magnitude). The entire page is enclosed within a double-line border.
</image-desc>

# 丈人之圖

<image-desc>
An astronomical diagram of the Zhangren (丈人, "Grandfather" or "Old Man") asterism, located in the Jing (井) lunar mansion. 
- The central axis contains the title "丈人之圖", the character "犬" (likely referring to the nearby "Dog" asterism), and the labels "金" (Metal/Venus) and "申宮" (Shen Palace). 
- Two solid black circles representing the stars are positioned centrally. 
- Data columns on the left and right provide technical specifications for two stars, including Magnitude (星躰), Mansion Degree (宿度), Latitude (緯度), and Longitude (經度).
</image-desc>

### 外屏之圖

<image-desc>A historical Chinese star chart representing the "Wai Ping" (Outer Screen) asterism. Seven solid black circles, representing stars, are arranged in a wide, shallow, horizontal arc that curves slightly downward in the middle. The stars are connected by a single continuous line. The title "外屏之圖" is written vertically on the right side. To the left of the stars, the label "外屏一" is followed by the characters "土木" below it. In the left margin, there is text indicating astronomical coordinates.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A rectangular bordered page from a Chinese astronomical text. At the top center is the heading character "天" (Heaven). Below it is a grid-like table with eight vertical column headers reading (from right to left): 經度 (Longitude), 緯度 (Latitude), 宿度 (Mansion Degree), 星林 (Star Forest/Catalog), 説全 (Complete Explanation), 經度, 緯度, and 宿度. The rightmost three columns are blank. The central columns contain the characters "土" and "酉宮" (Sign of the Rooster). The leftmost three columns contain numerical data: "五十一度四十五分" (51 degrees 45 minutes), "赤北三十一度五十六度" (Equator North 31 degrees 56 degrees), and "昴一十六度" (Pleiades 16 degrees).</image-desc>

# 咸池之圖

<image-desc>
A historical Chinese star chart depicting the "Xianchi" (Saline Pool) asterism and associated "Zhu" (Pillars) asterisms. 
- The title "咸池之圖" (Map of the Saline Pool) is written vertically in the upper right.
- At the top center, three stars arranged in a triangle are labeled "咸池".
- There are three distinct groups of stars labeled "柱" (Pillar). Each group contains three stars. One "柱" group is on the right, and two "柱" groups are positioned on the left side. 
- Stars are represented by circles, and lines connect them within the groups.
- Several characters resembling "七" (seven) are placed near the star groups and toward the bottom of the chart, likely serving as numerical or coordinate markers.
</image-desc>

## 天演之圖

->經度    緯度    宿度    星林<-

<image-desc>
A celestial diagram featuring five small circles representing stars, arranged in a descending diagonal line from the upper left toward the center of the frame. To the right of these stars are four vertical column headings intended for recording astronomical coordinates: 經度 (Longitude), 緯度 (Latitude), 宿度 (Lunar Mansion degree), and 星林 (Star Name/Cluster). The main title, "天演之圖" (Diagram of Celestial Evolution/Movement), is written vertically to the left of the headers.
</image-desc>

<vocab>天演之圖, 經度, 緯度, 宿度, 星林</vocab>

# 天園之圖

<image-desc>A traditional Chinese star chart depicting the "Tian Yuan" (Celestial Orchard) asterism. The diagram consists of thirteen circular dots representing stars, arranged in a winding, roughly horizontal path across the upper half of the framed area. Some dots are filled solid while others appear as outlines. The title "天園之圖" (Map of the Celestial Orchard) is written vertically on the right side. In the left margin, the character "畢" (Bì - the Net mansion) indicates the lunar mansion to which this asterism belongs.</image-desc>

<meta>The text on the left margin outside the frame indicates the section name '心' (Heart Mansion) and page number '二' (Two). The term '星躰' refers to the magnitude or appearance of the star. '大火' (Great Fire) is the traditional Chinese name for the star Antares (Alpha Scorpii), though it is labeled here as '心宿三' (Heart 3), whereas it is typically Heart 2. '積卒' (Jizu) is an asterism in the constellation Lupus or Centaurus; the marginal note specifies that it consists of twelve stars arranged in upper and lower rows.</meta>

緯度　赤南二十九度七分
宿度　牛三度
星躰　五等

<image-desc>A star chart for the asterism known as 天淵 (Tianyuan, the "Heavenly Abyss"). The diagram displays ten stars represented by small open circles. Eight of the stars are connected by dotted lines into two parallel vertical groupings. The group on the left consists of four stars in a rectangular formation with one star connected above and one below. The group on the right consists of four stars in a vertical line. Two additional unconnected stars are positioned on the far left of the diagram. To the right of the diagram, vertical text provides astronomical coordinates and magnitude data.</image-desc>

# 十二諸侯之圖

| 星躰 | 宿度 | 緯度 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 四等 | 女九度 | 亦南一十度四十七分 |

<image-desc>
A star chart depicting an asterism known as the "Twelve Feudal Lords" (十二諸侯). The diagram shows thirteen small circles representing stars (despite the title specifying twelve), each accompanied by a label corresponding to a historical Chinese state or a celestial body. 

The labels are organized roughly into four vertical columns:
- Far-left: 趙 (Zhao), 魏 (Wei), 韓 (Han), 晉 (Jin)
- Second column: 秦 (Qin), 周 (Zhou), 燕 (Yan)
- Third column: 月 (Moon), 楚 (Chu), 齊 (Qi)
- Rightmost column: 越 (Yue), 吳 (Wu), 鄭 (Zheng)

To the right of the diagram, three vertical columns of text describe the properties of these stars: they are of the 4th magnitude (星躰 四等), located at 9 degrees within the Nü (Maiden) lunar mansion (宿度 女九度), and have a latitude of 10 degrees 47 minutes south (緯度 亦南一十度四十七分).
</image-desc>

<vocab>十二諸侯, 星躰, 宿度, 緯度, 女宿, 四等</vocab>

# 天津之圖

星数九
一等

<image-desc>
A traditional Chinese astronomical chart for the asterism "Tianjin" (Celestial Ford), which corresponds to the modern constellation Cygnus. The diagram depicts nine stars represented as small circles, connected by dashed lines in a characteristic "V" or "U" shape. The labels identify the name of the asterism and provide data for its primary star, Tianjin 1 (Deneb). The text includes the number of stars (9), the magnitude (1st), the astrological associations (Metal/Water), the zodiacal palace (Zi/Aquarius), and the coordinates (Longitude 302° 25', Northern Latitude 59° 02').
</image-desc>

# 吳仲之圖

宿度
女十度

星等
三等

<image-desc>A celestial diagram of the "Wu Zhong" (吳仲) asterism. It features five small circles representing stars, connected by dotted lines into a pentagonal shape. Labels "吳仲" and "吳仲一" (Wu Zhong 1) are placed near the top star of the group. Underneath "吳仲一" is the term "金水" (Metal-Water). Below the entire constellation is the label "星宿" (Star Mansion/Asterism).</image-desc>

# 晉東上 <gloss>大</gloss>

<margin>子宮</margin>

| 經度 | 緯度 | 宿度 | 星等 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 三百一十六度三十六分 | 赤南二十三度二十六分 | 虛八度 | 六等 |

<margin>七</margin>
<margin>九</margin>

***

**Transcription Notes:**
*   **晉東上 (Jin Dong Shang):** This likely refers to a specific star designation in a traditional Chinese star catalog, potentially related to the "Twelve States" stars in the Aquarius/Capricorn region. The character **大** (Great/Large) appears as a sub-note or category indicator.
*   **子宮 (Palace of Zi):** This indicates the astrological sign/palace (roughly corresponding to Aquarius) where the star is located.
*   **Coordinates:** The longitude (**經度**) is recorded as 316 degrees 36 minutes, and the latitude (**緯度**) is 23 degrees 26 minutes south of the celestial equator (**赤南**). 
*   **宿度 (Mansion Degree):** The star is listed as being 8 degrees into the mansion of **虛** (Xu/The Void).
*   **星等 (Star Magnitude):** The star is listed as being of the 6th magnitude (**六等**).
*   **Marginalia:** The characters **七** (7) and **九** (9) appear in the outer left margins, likely indicating the page number 79.

<meta>
The page contains astronomical data for stars within the Li Yu (離瑜) asterism, part of the traditional Chinese star catalog. Coordinates are provided in Longitude (經度), Latitude (緯度), and Lunar Mansion degrees (宿度). The notation "赤南" indicates the star is south of the celestial equator (Red Circle). The mansion "Xu" (虛) is noted in the margin and the table. The character "少" (shao) in the mansion degree denotes a fraction (historically 1/4 or 0.25 degrees).
</meta>

***

**Transcription Note:** This page provides astronomical coordinates for stars within the lunar mansion **危** (Wei). The data includes **經度** (Longitude), **緯度** (Latitude), and **宿度** (Mansion degree) for the stars **虚梁二** (Xuliang 2) and **虚梁三** (Xuliang 3). The coordinates are specified relative to the **赤** (Red/Equatorial) system and the zodiacal sign **子宫** (Aquarius). The term "太" (Tai) in the mansion degree "危六度太" traditionally denotes an additional three-quarters of a degree.

# 羽林軍之圖

<image-desc>A traditional Chinese astronomical chart representing the "Yulinjun" (Imperial Guard) asterism. The diagram consists of numerous small circles representing individual stars, some of which are connected by short lines to form small groupings or constellations. The entire star field is positioned in the upper half of the frame, with the title text written vertically on the right side.</image-desc>

# 北落師門之圖

宿度 室一十五度

->北落師門<-
->上<-

<margin>乙</margin>
<margin>玄宮</margin>

經度 三百三十九度四分
緯度 赤南三十一度三十三分
宿度 室五度

<image-desc>
This page contains a vertical textual layout of astronomical data for the star Fomalhaut, known in Chinese as Beiluoshimen (北落師門). The central heading identifies it as a "Diagram of Beiluoshimen" (北落師門之圖). The data includes various coordinate measurements:
- Right side: Lunar mansion position (宿度) at 15 degrees of the Shi mansion (室) and star magnitude/class (星躔) as first class (一等).
- Left side: Longitude (經度) at 339 degrees 4 minutes; Latitude (緯度) at Equatorial South (赤南) 31 degrees 33 minutes; and an alternative mansion degree (宿度) at 5 degrees of the Shi mansion.
Additional notations include "Xuan Gong" (玄宮, the Black Palace or the northern quadrant of the sky) and navigational/classification marks like "Shang" (上) and "Yi" (乙).
</image-desc>

<vocab>北落師門, Fomalhaut, 宿度, 經度, 緯度, 玄宮, 星躔, 室宿</vocab>

The formation consists of 22 small circles representing stars, connected by a faint line in a long, winding serpentine shape. <meta>This page is from a Chinese star catalog, likely part of the 17th-century "Xiyang Xinfa Lishu" (西洋新法曆書) or a similar astronomical work. It lists coordinates for the 13th star of the Yi (翼, here written as 翌) mansion. The table provides two sets of coordinates (longitude, latitude, and mansion degrees), possibly representing different epochs or observations. The star is classified as having the nature of Venus and Mercury (金水) and is located in the zodiacal sign of the Snake (巳宮), with a magnitude of 4 (四等).</meta>
